Under the Hood: How `template` Is Built in Pure Ridgun (`src/main/ridgun/lib/template.rg`)\n\nOne of the biggest advantages of `ridgun-libs` is that **there are no C black boxes**. You can open `src/main/ridgun/lib/template.rg` and read exactly how every operation is implemented using pattern matching, `|` / `|.` guard trees, and tail-call optimization (TCO). Let's inspect the key building blocks of its real implementation:\n\n### 14.4.1. Internal Implementation: Internal Helpers\n\nObserve how `template.rg` declares explicit `fn :: ... => ...` contracts first and resolves each case through aligned equations and tail calls:\n\n```ridgun\nfn :: tm_lbrace => String\n tm_lbrace = char_from_code 123\n\nfn :: tm_rbrace => String\n tm_rbrace = char_from_code 125\n\nfn :: tm_braces => String\n tm_braces = (char_from_code 123) + (char_from_code 125)\n\nfn :: tm_is_space Int => Bool\n tm_is_space c\n | c == 32 = True\n | c == 9 = True\n | c == 10 = True\n | c == 13 = True\n | otherwise = False\n\nfn :: tm_trim_start String Int Int => Int\n tm_trim_start s idx n\n | idx >= n = n\n | tm_is_space (str_char_at s idx) = tm_trim_start s (idx + 1) n\n | otherwise = idx\n\nfn :: tm_trim_end String Int Int => Int\n tm_trim_end s start idx\n | idx <= start = start\n | tm_is_space (str_char_at s (idx - 1)) = tm_trim_end s start (idx - 1)\n | otherwise = idx\n\nfn :: tm_trim String => String\n tm_trim s =\n n = str_len s\n st = tm_trim_start s 0 n\n en = tm_trim_end s st n\n str_slice s st en\n\nfn :: tm_lookup [(String, String)] String => String\n tm_lookup [] _ = \"\"\n tm_lookup ((k, v):rest) key\n | k == key = v\n | otherwise = tm_lookup rest key\n\nfn :: tm_val_truthy String => Bool\n tm_val_truthy val\n | val == \"\" = False\n | val == \"false\" = False\n | val == \"False\" = False\n | val == \"0\" = False\n | otherwise = True\n```\n\n### 14.4.2. Internal Implementation: Public API\n\nObserve how `template.rg` declares explicit `fn :: ... => ...` contracts first and resolves each case through aligned equations and tail calls:\n\n```ridgun\nfn :: tmpl render with escape String [(String, String)] TmplEscape => String\n tmpl render with escape tmpl ctx mode = tm_scan tmpl 0 (str_len tmpl) ctx mode \"\"\n\nfn :: tmpl render String [(String, String)] => String\n tmpl render tmpl ctx = tmpl render with escape tmpl ctx TmplEscapeNone\n\nfn :: tmpl render html String [(String, String)] => String\n tmpl render html tmpl ctx = tmpl render with escape tmpl ctx TmplEscapeHtml\n\nfn :: tmpl render latex String [(String, String)] => String\n tmpl render latex tmpl ctx = tmpl render with escape tmpl ctx TmplEscapeLatex\n```\n\n---\n\n## 14.5. Algorithmic Guarantees, `RMM1`/`RMM2` Memory, and the 5 Golden Rules of `template`\n\n- **Algorithmic & Safety Guarantee**: Default XSS prevention in `tmpl render html` (escaping `&`, `<`, `>`, `\"`, `'`), while triple-stache `{{{var}}}` explicitly opts into raw trusted markup.\n- **$O(1)$ Stack Complexity via TCO**: Every recursive loop in `src/main/ridgun/lib/template.rg` is in tail position, so `ridgunc` compiles them into native jumps with zero stack growth.\n- **Deterministic Memory (`RMM1`/`RMM2`)**: Zero garbage-collector pauses; all intermediate allocations are reclaimed deterministically on scope exit.\n- **Continuous Verification**: Statically checked via `stable check` and tested by the automated suite in `src/test/ridgun/test template.rg`.\n\n### The 5 Golden Rules When Programming with `template`\n\n1. **Declare `import template` at the top of your file** and take advantage of space/underscore equivalence so your code reads like natural prose.\n2. **Align your data pipelines with `=`**: chain `template` calls into named intermediate steps and let `stable format` align the columns.\n3. **Leverage `template`'s total functions inside `|` guards**: inspect return values or unwrap ADTs via pattern matching rather than assuming external input is valid.\n4. **Avoid cloning large structures when only reading**: remember that under `RMM2`, function input arguments incur zero reference-count overhead (`+0` / `-0`).\n5. **Pair `template` with `test` (`import test`)**: always lock in your domain invariants with `assert equals` or `assert true`.\n\n---\n\nIn **Chapter 15**, we will explore the **`fmt`** lib in full depth (The `fmt` Lib — Canonical 2D Tabular Formatter and 9-Target Syntax Highlighter).\n"}, {"num": "14", "slug": "15-lib-fmt-tabular-formatter-and-highlighter", "file": "15-lib-fmt-tabular-formatter-and-highlighter.md", "title": "Chapter 15: The `fmt` Lib — Canonical 2D Tabular Formatter and 9-Target Syntax Highlighter", "short_title": "The `fmt` Lib — Canonical 2D Tabular Formatter and 9-Target Syntax Highlighter", "markdown": "# Chapter 15: The `fmt` Lib — Canonical 2D Tabular Formatter and 9-Target Syntax Highlighter\n\n## 15.1. Why Does the `fmt` Lib Exist? (The Real-World Problem & Ridgun Philosophy)\n\n> *\"Why do I need a `fmt` lib written from scratch in pure Ridgun instead of just linking a legacy C library?\"*\n\nThat is the first question every systems engineer asks when meeting `fmt`. And the answer captures the entire philosophy of `ridgun-libs`:\n\nLet's be honest: the beauty of Ridgun lies in its crisp tabular columns (`::`, `=`, `|`, `->`, `--`), but aligning spaces by hand or exporting syntax-highlighted code to ANSI terminals, HTML, and LaTeX without a dedicated engine is tedious.\n\nThe **`fmt`** lib is Ridgun's official 2D tabular formatter and lexical syntax highlighter: it aligns `fn ::` headers, equations, `|` guards, and `--` comments, and renders into **9 output targets** (`FmtPlain`, `FmtTty`, `FmtTrueColor`, `FmtHtmlInline`, `FmtHtmlCss`, `FmtMarkdown`, `FmtPango`, `FmtLatex`, `FmtRtf`).\n\nIn a traditional ecosystem, solving **`FMT`** tasks means pulling in external C dependencies, wrestling with raw pointers, opaque type conversions, unchecked exceptions, or unpredictable garbage-collector pauses. In Ridgun, the **`fmt`** lib (`src/main/ridgun/lib/fmt.rg`) tackles the problem with four non-negotiable principles:\n\n1. **Total Functions & Explicit Contracts (`=>`)**: no function in `fmt` throws hidden exceptions or relies on `null`. Every edge case (empty inputs, zero divisors, truncated frames, or out-of-bounds indices) returns a documented sentinel value or an algebraic sum type (`Option`, `Result`).\n2. **Zero C Code & Zero `libc`**: every line of `fmt` compiles directly into native ELF64 machine code verified by `ridgunc`.\n3. **Tail-Call Optimization (TCO)**: all internal loops in `fmt` are tail-recursive and compile into native hardware loops using $O(1)$ stack space.\n4. **Deterministic Memory (`RMM1` / `RMM2`)**: every intermediate allocation is reclaimed at the exact instruction where its last read finishes, with no Garbage Collector.\n\n---\n\n## 15.2. Native Artifacts, Imports, and Complete `fmt` API Catalog\n\nIn `ridgun-libs`, **`fmt`** is a first-class **lib** located at `src/main/ridgun/lib/fmt.rg` (domain `FMT`). When built with `stable bundle`, it compiles into three native artifacts:\n\n| Artifact | Path | Purpose |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Ridgun Bundle** | `target/bundles/fmt.rglib` | Portable distribution and direct `import fmt` resolution |\n| **Compiled Archive** | `target/fmt.rgc` | Type-checked intermediate artifact verified by `ridgunc` |\n| **ELF64 Shared Object** | `target/libfmt.so` | Native dynamic shared library with zero C or `libc` dependencies |\n\n> To use this lib in any `.rg` file, simply add `import fmt` at the top and call its functions using either multi-word spaces (`my fn x`) or underscores (`my_fn x`).\n\n### 15.2.1. Summary of `fmt` Functional Families\n\n| Signature / Function / Type | Purpose & Usage |\n|---|---|\n| `fmt source / fmt source with` | Format raw Ridgun source code using canonical 2D tabular column alignment |\n| `fmt check` | Return `True` if source code already matches canonical `fmt source` layout |\n| `fmt render / fmt render with` | Format and highlight source into any of the 9 `FmtTarget` backends |\n| `fmt tokenize line` | Tokenize a Ridgun line into `TokKeyword`, `TokType`, `TokOperator`, `TokString`, `TokNumber`, `TokComment` |\n\n### 15.2.2. Under the Hood: How `fmt` Is Built in Pure Ridgun (`src/main/ridgun/lib/fmt.rg`)\n\nOne of the biggest advantages of `ridgun-libs` is that **there are no C black boxes**. You can open `src/main/ridgun/lib/fmt.rg` and read exactly how every operation is implemented using pattern matching, `|` / `|.` guard trees, and tail-call optimization (TCO). Let's inspect the key building blocks of its real implementation:\n\n### 15.4.1. Internal Implementation: Configuration Constructors\n\nObserve how `fmt.rg` declares explicit `fn :: ... => ...` contracts first and resolves each case through aligned equations and tail calls:\n\n```ridgun\nfn :: default fmt config => FmtConfig\n      default fmt config =  FmtConfig 2 4 80 True FmtPlain FmtThemeNone\n\nfn :: fmt config Int Int Int Bool FmtTarget FmtTheme => FmtConfig\n      fmt config indent_w max_params max_w aligned target theme =\n        FmtConfig (fmt_max 1 indent_w) (fmt_max 0 max_params) (fmt_max 20 max_w) aligned target theme\n```\n\n### 15.4.2. Internal Implementation: Signature Parsing (`fn ::` / `op ::`)\n\nObserve how `fmt.rg` declares explicit `fn :: ... => ...` contracts first and resolves each case through aligned equations and tail calls:\n\n```ridgun\nfn :: fmt_split_sig_arrow [FmtToken] [String] => (Bool, [String], String, Int)\n      fmt_split_sig_arrow []         _        =  (False, [], \"\", 0)\n      fmt_split_sig_arrow ((FmtTok tok col):rest) lhs_acc\n        | tok == \"=>\" OR tok == \"->\" = (True, lhs_acc, tok, col)\n        | otherwise = fmt_split_sig_arrow rest (lhs_acc :: [tok])\n\nfn :: fmt_is_sig_type_tok String => Bool\n      fmt_is_sig_type_tok \"\"     =  False\n      fmt_is_sig_type_tok tok    =\n        ch = str_char_at tok 0\n        (ch >= 65 AND ch <= 90) OR ch == 91 OR ch == 40\n\nfn :: fmt_all_type_toks [String] => Bool\n      fmt_all_type_toks []       =  True\n      fmt_all_type_toks (t:rest)\n        | fmt_is_sig_type_tok t = fmt_all_type_toks rest\n        | otherwise = False\n\nfn :: fmt_is_bare_fn_lhs [String]       => Bool\n      fmt_is_bare_fn_lhs (first_w:rest)\n        | NOT (fmt_is_sig_type_tok first_w) = fmt_all_type_toks rest\n      fmt_is_bare_fn_lhs _              =  False\n\nfn :: fmt_infer_bare_kind [String] => String\n      fmt_infer_bare_kind lhs_strs\n        | fmt_is_bare_fn_lhs lhs_strs = \"bare_fn\"\n        | otherwise = \"bare_op\"\n\nfn :: fmt_parse_sig_line String => FmtSig\n      fmt_parse_sig_line line   =\n        clean = fmt_strip_comment line\n        cmt = fmt_extract_comment line\n        toks = fmt_scan_tokens clean\n        fmt_parse_sig_tokens (fmt_leading_indent line) clean cmt toks\n\nfn :: fmt_parse_sig_tokens Int String String [FmtToken] => FmtSig\n      fmt_parse_sig_tokens _   clean  cmt    ((FmtTok kind _):(FmtTok \"::\" _):rest)\n        | kind == \"fn\" OR kind == \"op\" = fmt_finish_sig_parse clean cmt kind (fmt_split_sig_arrow rest [])\n      fmt_parse_sig_tokens 0   clean  cmt    ((FmtTok w col):rest)\n        | w == \"type\" OR w == \"expand\" OR w == \"import\" OR w == \"global\" OR w == \"context\" OR w == \"shared\" OR w == \"hardware\" OR (str_starts_with w \"@\") OR (str_starts_with w \"--\") OR (str_starts_with w \"|\") = NoSig\n        | otherwise = fmt_finish_bare_sig_parse clean cmt (fmt_split_sig_arrow ([FmtTok w col] :: rest) [])\n      fmt_parse_sig_tokens _   _      _      _          =  NoSig\n\nfn :: fmt_finish_bare_sig_parse String String (Bool, [String], String, Int) => FmtSig\n      fmt_finish_bare_sig_parse _ _ (False, _, _, _) = NoSig\n      fmt_finish_bare_sig_parse _ _ (True, [], _, _) = NoSig\n      fmt_finish_bare_sig_parse clean cmt (True, lhs_strs, arrow_s, arrow_col)\n        | arrow_s == \"=>\" = fmt_finish_sig_parse clean cmt (fmt_infer_bare_kind lhs_strs) (True, lhs_strs, arrow_s, arrow_col)\n        | otherwise = NoSig\n```\n\n### 15.4.3. Internal Implementation: Lexical Token Classification & Syntax Highlighting\n\nObserve how `fmt.rg` declares explicit `fn :: ... => ...` contracts first and resolves each case through aligned equations and tail calls:\n\n```ridgun\nfn :: fmt_is_digit Int => Bool\n      fmt_is_digit c   =  c >= 48 AND c <= 57\n\nfn :: fmt_is_upper Int => Bool\n      fmt_is_upper c   =  c >= 65 AND c <= 90\n\nfn :: fmt_is_lower Int => Bool\n      fmt_is_lower c   =  c >= 97 AND c <= 122\n\nfn :: fmt_is_ident_char Int => Bool\n      fmt_is_ident_char c   =  (fmt_is_upper c) OR (fmt_is_lower c) OR (fmt_is_digit c) OR c == 95\n\nfn :: fmt_is_keyword String => Bool\n      fmt_is_keyword w      =\n        w == \"fn\" OR w == \"op\" OR w == \"type\" OR w == \"expand\" OR w == \"import\" OR w == \"module\" OR w == \"where\" OR w == \"otherwise\" OR w == \"with\" OR w == \"do\" OR w == \"mut\" OR w == \"in\" OR w == \"agent\" OR w == \"send\" OR w == \"to\" OR w == \"receive\" OR w == \"from\" OR w == \"try\" OR w == \"stop\" OR w == \"timeout\" OR w == \"global\" OR w == \"context\" OR w == \"shared\" OR w == \"hardware\" OR w == \"atomic\" OR w == \"region\" OR w == \"limit\" OR w == \"map\" OR w == \"filter\" OR w == \"fold\"\n\nfn :: fmt_is_logic_op String => Bool\n      fmt_is_logic_op w      =\n        w == \"AND\" OR w == \"OR\" OR w == \"XOR\" OR w == \"NOT\" OR w == \"and\" OR w == \"or\" OR w == \"xor\" OR w == \"not\" OR w == \"shl\" OR w == \"shr\" OR w == \"rotr\" OR w == \"rotl\"\n\nfn :: fmt_is_bool_lit String => Bool\n      fmt_is_bool_lit w      =  w == \"True\" OR w == \"False\"\n\nfn :: fmt_classify_word String Bool  => String\n      fmt_classify_word \"\"     _     =  \"plain\"\n      fmt_classify_word w      in_fn\n        | fmt_is_keyword w = \"keyword\"\n        | fmt_is_logic_op w = \"logic\"\n        | fmt_is_bool_lit w = \"bool\"\n        | fmt_is_upper (str_char_at w 0) = \"type\"\n        | fmt_is_digit (str_char_at w 0) = \"number\"\n        | in_fn = \"function\"\n        | otherwise = \"plain\"\n```\n\n### 15.4.4. Internal Implementation: Public Entry Points\n\nObserve how `fmt.rg` declares explicit `fn :: ... => ...` contracts first and resolves each case through aligned equations and tail Algorithmic Guarantees, `RMM1`/`RMM2` Memory, and the 5 Golden Rules of `fmt`\n\n- **Algorithmic & Safety Guarantee**: Strict idempotence (`fmt source (fmt source s) == fmt source s`) and 100% preservation of semantic tokens.\n- **$O(1)$ Stack Complexity via TCO**: Every recursive loop in `src/main/ridgun/lib/fmt.rg` is in tail position, so `ridgunc` compiles them into native jumps with zero stack growth.\n- **Deterministic Memory (`RMM1`/`RMM2`)**: Zero garbage-collector pauses; all intermediate allocations are reclaimed deterministically on scope exit.\n- **Continuous Verification**: Statically checked via `stable check` and tested by the automated suite in `src/test/ridgun/test fmt.rg`.\n\n### The 5 Golden Rules When Programming with `fmt`\n\n1. **Declare `import fmt` at the top of your file** and take advantage of space/underscore equivalence so your code reads like natural prose.\n2. **Align your data pipelines with `=`**: chain `fmt` calls into named intermediate steps and let `stable format` align the columns.\n3. **Leverage `fmt`'s total functions inside `|` guards**: inspect return values or unwrap ADTs via pattern matching rather than assuming external input is valid.\n4. **Avoid cloning large structures when only reading**: remember that under `RMM2`, function input arguments incur zero reference-count overhead (`+0` / `-0`).\n5. **Pair `fmt` with `test` (`import test`)**: always lock in your domain invariants with `assert equals` or `assert true`.\n\n---\n\nIn **Chapter 16**, we will explore the **`cli`** lib in full depth (The `cli` Lib — Flags, Options, `--` Barrier, Subcommands, and Typed Env Readers).\n"}, {"num": "15", "slug": "16-lib-cli-flags-options-and-env", "file": "16-lib-cli-flags-options-and-env.md", "title": "Chapter 16: The `cli` Lib — Flags, Options, `--` Barrier, Subcommands, and Typed Env Readers", "short_title": "The `cli` Lib — Flags, Options, `--` Barrier, Subcommands, and Typed Env Readers", "markdown": "# Chapter 16: The `cli` Lib — Flags, Options, `--` Barrier, Subcommands, and Typed Env Readers\n\n## 16.1. Why Does the `cli` Lib Exist? (The Real-World Problem & Ridgun Philosophy)\n\n> *\"Why do I need a `cli` lib written from scratch in pure Ridgun instead of just linking a legacy C library?\"*\n\nThat is the first question every systems engineer asks when meeting `cli`. And the answer captures the entire philosophy of `ridgun-libs`:\n\nLet's be honest: hand-rolling `argv` parsing always starts as an innocent loop and breaks the moment a user passes bundled `-la` flags, `--port=9090`, or `--` followed by a filename starting with a dash.\n\nThe **`cli`** lib handles long flags (`--verbose`), short flags (`-v`), bundled short flags (`-la`), valued options (`--port=8080`, `--port 8080`, `-p 8080`), the POSIX `--` end-of-options barrier, positional arguments, subcommands, and typed environment variable readers.\n\nIn a traditional ecosystem, solving **`CLI`** tasks means pulling in external C dependencies, wrestling with raw pointers, opaque type conversions, unchecked exceptions, or unpredictable garbage-collector pauses. In Ridgun, the **`cli`** lib (`src/main/ridgun/lib/cli.rg`) tackles the problem with four non-negotiable principles:\n\n1. **Total Functions & Explicit Contracts (`=>`)**: no function in `cli` throws hidden exceptions or relies on `null`. Every edge case (empty inputs, zero divisors, truncated frames, or out-of-bounds indices) returns a documented sentinel value or an algebraic sum type (`Option`, `Result`).\n2. **Zero C Code & Zero `libc`**: every line of `cli` compiles directly into native ELF64 machine code verified by `ridgunc`.\n3. **Tail-Call Optimization (TCO)**: all internal loops in `cli` are tail-recursive and compile into native hardware loops using $O(1)$ stack space.\n4. **Deterministic Memory (`RMM1` / `RMM2`)**: every intermediate allocation is reclaimed at the exact instruction where its last read finishes, with no Garbage Collector.\n\n---\n\n## 16.2. Native Artifacts, Imports, and Complete `cli` API Catalog\n\nIn `ridgun-libs`, **`cli`** is a first-class **lib** located at `src/main/ridgun/lib/cli.rg` (domain `CLI`). When built with `stable bundle`, it compiles into three native artifacts:\n\n| Artifact | Path | Purpose |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Ridgun Bundle** | `target/bundles/cli.rglib` | Portable distribution and direct `import cli` resolution |\n| **Compiled Archive** | `target/cli.rgc` | Type-checked intermediate artifact verified by `ridgunc` |\n| **ELF64 Shared Object** | `target/libcli.so` | Native dynamic shared library with zero C or `libc` dependencies |\n\n> To use this lib in any `.rg` file, simply add `import cli` at the top and call its functions using either multi-word spaces (`my fn x`) or underscores (`my_fn x`).\n\n### 16.2.1. Summary of `cli` Functional Families\n\n| Signature / Function / Type | Purpose & Usage |\n|---|---|\n| `cli has flag : [String] -> String -> String -> Bool` | Detect `--long`, `-s`, or bundled `-xyz` before `--` |\n| `cli get opt / cli get int opt` | Extract string or integer option (`--k=v`, `--k v`, `-k v`) with default |\n| `cli positionals / cli subcommand` | Collect non-flag positional arguments (including everything after `--`) |\n| `env get or / env get int or / env get bool or` | Read environment variables with typed fallback defaults |\n\n### 16.2.2. Under the Hood: How `cli` Is Built in Pure Ridgun (`src/main/ridgun/lib/cli.rg`)\n\nOne of the biggest advantages of `ridgun-libs` is that **there are no C black boxes**. Internal Implementation: Internal String & List Helpers\n\nObserve how `cli.rg` declares explicit `fn :: ... => ...` contracts first and resolves each case through aligned equations and tail calls:\n\n```ridgun\nfn :: cl str contains char String Int Int Int => Bool\n      cl str contains char s ch idx len =\n        | idx >= len                = False\n        | (str char at s idx) == ch = True\n        | otherwise                 = cl str contains char s ch (idx + 1) len\n\nfn :: cl is bundled short String String => Bool\n      cl is bundled short arg short_flag =\n        | (str len short_flag) != 2        = False\n        | (str char at short_flag 0) != 45 = False\n        | (str len arg) < 2                = False\n        | (str char at arg 0) != 45        = False\n        | (str char at arg 1) == 45        = False\n        | otherwise                        = cl str contains char arg (str char at short_flag 1) 1 (str len arg)\n\nfn :: cl list contains str [String] String => Bool\n      cl list contains str [] _ = False\n      cl list contains str (x:rest) tgt =\n        | x == tgt  = True\n        | otherwise = cl list contains str rest tgt\n\nfn :: cl is digits loop String Int Int => Bool\n      cl is digits loop s idx len =\n        | idx >= len                                           = True\n        | (str char at s idx) < 48 or (str char at s idx) > 57 = False\n        | otherwise                                            = cl is digits loop s (idx + 1) len\n\nfn :: cl is signed int len String Int => Bool\n      cl is signed int len s n =\n        | n == 0                  = False\n        | (str char at s 0) == 45 =\n          | n <= 1    = False\n          | otherwise = cl is digits loop s 1 n |.\n        | otherwise               = cl is digits loop s 0 n\n\nfn :: cl is signed int String => Bool\n      cl is signed int s      =  cl is signed int len s (str len s)\n\nfn :: cl is ws Int => Bool\n      cl is ws c   =  c == 32 or c == 9 or c == 10 or c == 13\n\nfn :: cl lower char String Int Int => String\n      cl lower char s idx c =\n        | c >= 65 and c <= 90 = char from code (c + 32)\n        | otherwise           = str slice s idx (idx + 1)\n```\n\n### 16.4.2. Internal Implementation: Positional Arguments & Subcommand Extraction\n\nObserve how `cli.rg` declares explicit `fn :: ... => ...` contracts first and resolves each case through aligned equations and tail calls:\n\n```ridgun\nfn :: cl is flag tok String => Bool\n      cl is flag tok a      =\n        | (str len a) <= 1        = False\n        | (str char at a 0) == 45 = True\n        | otherwise               = False\n\nfn :: cli positionals with opts [String] [String] => [String]\n      cli positionals with opts [] _ = []\n      cli positionals with opts (a:[]) _ =\n        | a == \"--\"        = []\n        | cl is flag tok a = []\n        | otherwise        = [a]\n      cli positionals with opts (a:b:rst) val_opts =\n        | a == \"--\"                       = [b] :: rst\n        | cl list contains str val_opts a = cli positionals with opts rst val_opts\n        | cl is flag tok a                = cli positionals with opts ([b] :: rst) val_opts\n        | otherwise                       = [a] :: (cli positionals with opts ([b] :: rst) val_opts)\n\nfn :: cli positionals [String] => [String]\n      cli positionals args     =  cli positionals with opts args []\n\nfn :: cl first str [String] => String\n      cl first str []       =  \"\"\n      cl first str (x:_)    =  x\n\nfn :: cli subcommand [String] => String\n      cli subcommand args     =  cl first str (cli positionals args)\n```\n\n### 16.4.3. Internal Implementation: Typed Environment Variable Readers\n\nObserve how `cli.rg` declares explicit `fn :: ... => ...` contracts first and resolves each case through aligned equations and tail calls:\n\n```ridgun\nfn :: cl env val or String String => String\n      cl env val or val fallback =\n        | (str len val) == 0 = fallback\n        | otherwise          = val\n\nfn :: env get or String String   => String\n      env get or key    fallback =  cl env val or (sys getenv key) fallback\n\nfn :: env get int or String Int => Int\n      env get int or key fallback = cl parse int opt (sys getenv key) fallback\n\nfn :: cl parse bool val String Bool => Bool\n      cl parse bool val val fallback =\n        | val == \"1\" or val == \"true\" or val == \"yes\" or val == \"on\"  = True\n        | val == \"0\" or val == \"false\" or val == \"no\" or val == \"off\" = False\n        | otherwise                                                   = fallback\n\nfn :: env get bool or String Bool => Bool\n      env get bool or key fallback =\n        raw = sys getenv key\n        cl parse bool val (cl trim lower loop raw 0 (str len raw)) fallback\n```\n\n---\n\n## 16.5. Algorithmic Guarantees, `RMM1`/`RMM2` Memory, and the 5 Golden Rules of `cli`\n\n- **Algorithmic & Safety Guarantee**: Strict adherence to the POSIX `--` barrier: no token after `--` is ever misinterpreted as a flag or option.\n- **$O(1)$ Stack Complexity via TCO**: Every recursive loop in `src/main/ridgun/lib/cli.rg` is in tail position, so `ridgunc` compiles them into native jumps with zero stack growth.\n- **Deterministic Memory (`RMM1`/`RMM2`)**: Zero garbage-collector pauses; all intermediate allocations are reclaimed deterministically on scope exit.\n- **Continuous Verification**: Statically checked via `stable check` and tested by the automated suite in `src/test/ridgun/test cli.rg`.\n\n### The 5 Golden Rules When Programming with `cli`\n\n1. **Declare `import cli` at the top of your file** and take advantage of space/underscore equivalence so your code reads like natural prose.\n2. **Align your data pipelines with `=`**: chain `cli` calls into named intermediate steps and let `stable format` align the columns.\n3. **Leverage `cli`'s total functions inside `|` guards**: inspect return values or unwrap ADTs via pattern matching rather than assuming external input is valid.\n4. **Avoid cloning large structures when only reading**: remember that under `RMM2`, function input arguments incur zero reference-count overhead (`+0` / `-0`).\n5. **Pair `cli` with `test` (`import test`)**: always lock in your domain invariants with `assert equals` or `assert true`.\n\n---\n\nIn **Chapter 17**, we will explore the **`path`** lib in full depth (The `path` Lib — POSIX Normalization (`path clean`) and Path-Traversal Confinement).\n"}, {"num": "16", "slug": "17-lib-path-posix-paths-and-confinement", "file": "17-lib-path-posix-paths-and-confinement.md", "title": "Chapter 17: The `path` Lib — POSIX Normalization (`path clean`) and Path-Traversal Confinement", "short_title": "The `path` Lib — POSIX Normalization (`path clean`) and Path-Traversal Confinement", "markdown": "# Chapter 17: The `path` Lib — POSIX Normalization (`path clean`) and Path-Traversal Confinement\n\n## 17.1. Why Does the `path` Lib Exist? (The Real-World Problem & Ridgun Philosophy)\n\n> *\"Why do I need a `path` lib written from scratch in pure Ridgun instead of just linking a legacy C library?\"*\n\nThat is the first question every systems engineer asks when meeting `path`. And the answer captures the entire philosophy of `ridgun-libs`:\n\nLet's be honest: joining file paths with `base + \"/\" + user_path` without lexical `..` resolution and boundary confinement is an open invitation for a `../../etc/passwd` path-traversal exploit.\n\nThe **`path`** lib implements POSIX lexical normalization (`path clean`), safe joining (`path join`), component extraction (`dirname`, `basename`, `stem`, `ext`, `replace ext`), and the **`path is safe subpath`** (`VUL-INJ-001`) directory-confinement guard.\n\nIn a traditional ecosystem, solving **`PATH`** tasks means pulling in external C dependencies, wrestling with raw pointers, opaque type conversions, unchecked exceptions, or unpredictable garbage-collector pauses. In Ridgun, the **`path`** lib (`src/main/ridgun/lib/path.rg`) tackles the problem with four non-negotiable principles:\n\n1. **Total Functions & Explicit Contracts (`=>`)**: no function in `path` throws hidden exceptions or relies on `null`. Every edge case (empty inputs, zero divisors, truncated frames, or out-of-bounds indices) returns a documented sentinel value or an algebraic sum type (`Option`, `Result`).\n2. **Zero C Code & Zero `libc`**: every line of `path` compiles directly into native ELF64 machine code verified by `ridgunc`.\n3. **Tail-Call Optimization (TCO)**: all internal loops in `path` are tail-recursive and compile into native hardware loops using $O(1)$ stack space.\n4. **Deterministic Memory (`RMM1` / `RMM2`)**: every intermediate allocation is reclaimed at the exact instruction where its last read finishes, with no Garbage Collector.\n\n---\n\n## 17.2. Native Artifacts, Imports, and Complete `path` API Catalog\n\nIn `ridgun-libs`, **`path`** is a first-class **lib** located at `src/main/ridgun/lib/path.rg` (domain `PATH`). When built with `stable bundle`, it compiles into three native artifacts:\n\n| Artifact | Path | Purpose |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Ridgun Bundle** | `target/bundles/path.rglib` | Portable distribution and direct `import path` resolution |\n| **Compiled Archive** | `target/path.rgc` | Type-checked intermediate artifact verified by `ridgunc` |\n| **ELF64 Shared Object** | `target/libpath.so` | Native dynamic shared library with zero C or `libc` dependencies |\n\n> To use this lib in any `.rg` file, simply add `import path` at the top and call its functions using either multi-word spaces (`my fn x`) or underscores (`my_fn x`).\n\n### 17.2.1. Summary of `path` Functional Families\n\n| Signature / Function / Type | Purpose & Usage |\n|---|---|\n| `path clean / path join / path join list` | Collapse `//`, `.`, and `..` lexically and join path segments |\n| `path dirname / path basename / path split` | Split a path into parent directory and final filename component |\n| `path ext / path stem / path replace ext` | Extract or swap file extensions (correctly treating `.bashrc` as stem `.bashrc`, ext `\"\"`) |\n| `path is safe subpath : String -> String -> Bool` | Verify `rel` stays strictly inside `base_dir` and has no NUL bytes |\n\n### 17.2.2.  _ = assert str eq (path join list [\"src\", \"main\", \"ridgun\", \"lib\", \"path.rg\"]) \"src/main/ridgun/lib/path.rg\" \"AC1: join_list 5 segments\"\n        ()\n```\n\n### 17.3.4. Under the Hood: How `path` Is Built in Pure Ridgun (`src/main/ridgun/lib/path.rg`)\n\nOne of the biggest advantages of `ridgun-libs` is that **there are no C black boxes**. You can open `src/main/ridgun/lib/path.rg` and read exactly how every operation is implemented using pattern matching, `|` / `|.` guard trees, and tail-call optimization (TCO). Let's inspect the key building blocks of its real implementation:\n\n### 17.4.1. Internal Implementation: Core Implementation Part 1\n\nObserve how `path.rg` declares explicit `fn :: ... => ...` contracts first and resolves each case through aligned equations and tail calls:\n\n```ridgun\nfn :: pa split slash loop String Int Int Int => [String]\n      pa split slash loop p start idx len =\n        | idx >= len                = [(str slice p start len)]\n        | (str char at p idx) == 47 = [(str slice p start idx)] :: (pa split slash loop p (idx + 1) (idx + 1) len)\n        | otherwise                 = pa split slash loop p start (idx + 1) len\n\nfn :: pa rev strs [String] [String] => [String]\n      pa rev strs []       acc      =  acc\n      pa rev strs (x:rest) acc      =  pa rev strs rest ([x] :: acc)\n\nfn :: pa join slash [String] => String\n      pa join slash []       =  \"\"\n      pa join slash (x:[])   =  x\n      pa join slash (x:rest) =  x + \"/\" + (pa join slash rest)\n\nfn :: pa pop stack [String] Bool  => [String]\n      pa pop stack []       True  =  []\n      pa pop stack []       False =  [\"..\"]\n      pa pop stack (top:rs) is_ab =\n        | top == \"..\" = [\"..\", \"..\"] :: rs\n        | otherwise   = rs\n\nfn :: pa norm segs [String] [String] Bool => [String]\n      pa norm segs [] stack _ = pa rev strs stack []\n      pa norm segs (s:rest) stack is_ab =\n        | (str len s) == 0 or s == \".\" = pa norm segs rest stack is_ab\n        | s == \"..\"                    = pa norm segs rest (pa pop stack stack is_ab) is_ab\n        | otherwise                    = pa norm segs rest ([s] :: stack) is_ab\n\nfn :: pa finish clean String Bool  => String\n      pa finish clean joined True  =\n        | (str len joined) == 0 = \"/\"\n        | otherwise             = \"/\" + joined\n      pa finish clean joined False =\n        | (str len joined) == 0 = \".\"\n        | otherwise             = joined\n```\n\n### 17.4.2. Internal Implementation: Core Implementation Part 2\n\nObserve how `path.rg` declares explicit `fn :: ... => ...` contracts first and resolves each case through aligned equations and tail calls:\n\n```ridgun\nfn :: path is abs String => Bool\n      path is abs p      =\n        | (str len p) == 0        = False\n        | (str char at p 0) == 47 = True\n        | otherwise               = False\n\nfn :: path is rel String => Bool\n      path is rel p      =  not (path is abs p)\n\nfn :: path clean String => String\n      path clean p      =\n        | (str len p) == 0 = \".\"\n        | otherwise =\n          is_ab = path is abs p\n          segs  = pa split slash loop p 0 0 (str len p)\n          norm  = pa norm segs segs [] is_ab\n          pa finish clean (pa join slash norm) is_ab\n\nfn :: path join String String => String\n      path join a      b      =\n        | (str len a) == 0 = path clean b\n        | (str len b) == 0 = path clean a\n        | path is abs b    = path clean b\n        | otherwise        = path clean (a + \"/\" + b)\n\nfn :: path join list [String]   => String\n      path join list []         =  \".\"\n      path join list (x:[])     =  path clean x\n      path join list (x:y:rest) =  path join list ([(path join x y)] :: rest)\n\nfn :: pa last char idx String Int Int => Int\n      pa last char idx s ch idx =\n        | idx < 0                   = 0 - 1\n        | (str char at s idx) == ch = idx\n        | otherwise                 = pa last char idx s ch (idx - 1)\n```\n\n### 17.4.3. Internal Implementation: Core Implementation Part 4\n\nObserve how `path.rg` declares explicit `fn :: ... => ...` contracts first and resolves each case through aligned equations and tail calls:\n\n```ridgun\nfn :: path ext String => String\n      path ext p      =\n        base = path basename p\n        pa ext at base (pa last char idx base 46 ((str len base) - 1))\n\nfn :: pa stem at String Int => String\n      pa stem at base   dot =\n        | dot <= 0  = base\n        | otherwise = str slice base 0 dot\n\nfn :: path stem String => String\n      path stem p      =\n        base = path basename p\n        pa stem at base (pa last char idx base 46 ((str len base) - 1))\n\nfn :: pa norm ext String => String\n      pa norm ext ext    =\n        | (str len ext) == 0        = \"\"\n        | (str char at ext 0) == 46 = ext\n        | otherwise                 = \".\" + ext\n\nfn :: pa combine dir base String String => String\n      pa combine dir base dir base =\n        | dir == \".\" = base\n        | dir == \"/\" = \"/\" + base\n        | otherwise  = dir + \"/\" + base\n\nfn :: path replace ext String String  => String\n      path replace ext p      new_ext =\n        pa combine dir base (path dirname p) ((path stem p) + (pa norm ext new_ext))\n\nfn :: pa is safe char Int => Bool\n      pa is safe char c   =\n        | c >= 48 and c <= 57                        = True\n        | c >= 65 and c <= 90                        = True\n        | c >= 97 and c <= 122                       = True\n        | c == 47 or c == 46 or c == 95 or c == 45   = True\n        | otherwise                                  = False\n```\n\n### 17.4.4. Internal Implementation: Core Implementation Part 5\n\nObserve how `path.rg` declares explicit `fn :: ... => ...` contracts first and resolves each case through aligned equations and tail calls:\n\n```ridgun\nfn :: pa safe chars loop String Int Int => Bool\n      pa safe chars loop s idx len =\n        | idx >= len                          = True\n        | not (pa is safe char (str char at s idx)) = False\n        | otherwise                           = pa safe chars loop s (idx + 1) len\n\nfn :: pa check prefix String String => Bool\n      pa check prefix bclean full   =\n        | bclean == \"/\" = path is abs full\n        | full == bclean = True\n        | otherwise     = str starts with full (bclean + \"/\")\n\nfn :: path is safe subpath String String => Bool\n      path is safe subpath base child =\n        | (str len base) == 0 or (str len child) == 0          = False\n        | path is abs child                                    = False\n        | not (pa safe chars loop base 0 (str len base))       = False\n        | not (pa safe chars loop child 0 (str len child))     = False\n        | otherwise =\n          bclean = path clean base\n          full   = path clean (bclean + \"/\" + child)\n          pa check prefix bclean full\n```\n\n---\n\n## 17.5. Algorithmic Guarantees, `RMM1`/`RMM2` Memory, and the 5 Golden Rules of `path`\n\n- **Algorithmic & Safety Guarantee**: `path is safe subpath` rejects absolute paths, NUL bytes, and any `..` sequence that attempts to escape `base_dir` or bleed into a sibling prefix (`/srv/www-secret`).\n- **$O(1)$ Stack Complexity via TCO**: Every recursive loop in `src/main/ridgun/lib/path.rg` is in tail position, so `ridgunc` compiles them into native jumps with zero stack growth.\n- **Deterministic Memory (`RMM1`/`RMM2`)**: Zero garbage-collector pauses; all intermediate allocations are reclaimed deterministically on scope exit.\n- **Continuous Verification**: Statically checked via `stable check` and tested by the automated suite in `src/test/ridgun/test path.rg`.\n\n### The 5 Golden Rules When Programming with `path`\n\n1. **Declare `import path` at the top of your file** and take advantage of space/underscore equivalence so your code reads like natural prose.\n2. **Align your data pipelines with `=`**: chain `path` calls into named intermediate steps and let `stable format` align the columns.\n3. **Leverage `path`'s total functions inside `|` guards**: inspect return values or unwrap ADTs via pattern matching rather than assuming external input is valid.\n4. **Avoid cloning large structures when only reading**: remember that under `RMM2`, function input arguments incur zero reference-count overhead (`+0` / `-0`).\n5. **Pair `path` with `test` (`import test`)**: always lock in your domain invariants with `assert equals` or `assert true`.\n\n---\n\nIn **Chapter 18**, we will explore the **`time`** lib in full depth (The `time` Lib — Howard Hinnant UTC Calendar, ISO-8601, Durations, and Backoff).\n"}, {"num": "17", "slug": "18-lib-time-utc-calendar-iso8601-and-backoff", "file": "18-lib-time-utc-calendar-iso8601-and-backoff.md", "title": "Chapter 18: The `time` Lib — Howard Hinnant UTC Calendar, ISO-8601, Durations, and Backoff", "short_title": "The `time` Lib — Howard Hinnant UTC Calendar, ISO-8601, Durations, and Backoff", "markdown": "# Chapter 18: The `time` Lib — Howard Hinnant UTC Calendar, ISO-8601, Durations, and Backoff\n\n## 18.1. Why Does the `time` Lib Exist? (The Real-World Problem & Ridgun Philosophy)\n\n> *\"Why do I need a `time` lib written from scratch in pure Ridgun instead of just linking a legacy C library?\"*\n\nThat is the first question every systems engineer asks when meeting `time`. And the answer captures the entire philosophy of `ridgun-libs`:\n\nLet's be honest: `libc` date functions (`localtime`, `gmtime`, `mktime`) rely on hidden mutable global state (`TZ`), aren't thread-safe, and trip developers up on century leap-year rules.\n\nThe **`time`** lib implements **Howard Hinnant's $O(1)$** proleptic Gregorian UTC civil calendar algorithm, strict **ISO-8601 / RFC 3339** formatting and parsing (`YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ`), human-readable durations (`1h 1m 1s 5ms`), and bounded exponential backoff.\n\nIn a traditional ecosystem, solving **`TIME`** tasks means pulling in external C dependencies, wrestling with raw pointers, opaque type conversions, unchecked exceptions, or unpredictable garbage-collector pauses. In Ridgun, the **`time`** lib (`src/main/ridgun/lib/time.rg`) tackles the problem with four non-negotiable principles:\n\n1. **Total Functions & Explicit Contracts (`=>`)**: no function in `time` throws hidden exceptions or relies on `null`. Every edge case (empty inputs, zero divisors, truncated frames, or out-of-bounds indices) returns a documented sentinel value or an algebraic sum type (`Option`, `Result`).\n2. **Zero C Code & Zero `libc`**: every line of `time` compiles directly into native ELF64 machine code verified by `ridgunc`.\n3. **Tail-Call Optimization (TCO)**: all internal loops in `time` are tail-recursive and compile into native hardware loops using $O(1)$ stack space.\n4. **Deterministic Memory (`RMM1` / `RMM2`)**: every intermediate allocation is reclaimed at the exact instruction where its last read finishes, with no Garbage Collector.\n\n---\n\n## 18.2. Native Artifacts, Imports, and Complete `time` API Catalog\n\nIn `ridgun-libs`, **`time`** is a first-class **lib** located at `src/main/ridgun/lib/time.rg` (domain `TIME`). When built with `stable bundle`, it compiles into three native artifacts:\n\n| Artifact | Path | Purpose |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Ridgun Bundle** | `target/bundles/time.rglib` | Portable distribution and direct `import time` resolution |\n| **Compiled Archive** | `target/time.rgc` | Type-checked intermediate artifact verified by `ridgunc` |\n| **ELF64 Shared Object** | `target/libtime.so` | Native dynamic shared library with zero C or `libc` dependencies |\n\n> To use this lib in any `.rg` file, simply add `import time` at the top and call its functions using either multi-word spaces (`my fn x`) or underscores (`my_fn x`).\n\n### 18.2.1. Summary of `time` Functional Families\n\n| Signature / Function / Type | Purpose & Usage |\n|---|---|\n| `time epoch to utc / time utc to epoch` | $O(1)$ conversion between Unix epoch seconds and `(year, month, day, hour, min, sec)` |\n| `time is leap year / time days in month / time day of week` | Leap-year rules (`400 / 100 / 4`), month lengths, and ISO weekday (`1=Mon..7=Sun`) |\n| `time format iso8601 / time parse iso8601` | Format and validate `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ` timestamps (`-1` on invalid dates like Feb 30) |\n| `time format duration ms / time backoff ms` | Human duration strings and capped $base \\cdot 2^{attempt}$ retry delays |\n\n### 18.2.2. Exhaustive Catalog of Types and Signatures in `src/main/ridgun/lib/time.rg`\n\nBelow is the complete inventory of algebraic data types (`type`), mixfix operators (`op ::`), and function signatures (`fn ::`) defined in `src/main/ridgun/lib/time.rg` (`0` types, `0` operators, and `26` functions):\n\n| # | Function / Signature (`fn ::`) | Return Type |\n|---|---|---|\n| `1` | `fn :: time is leap year Int` | `Bool` |\n| `2` | `fn :: tm feb days Int` | `Int` |\n| `3` | `fn :: time days in month Int Int` | `Int` |\n| `4` | `fn :: tm adj year Int Int` | `Int` |\n| `5` | `fn :: tm calc era Int` | `Int` |\n| `6` | `fn :: tm calc mp Int` | `Int` |\n| `7` | `fn :: tm days from civil Int Int Int` | `Int` |\n| `8` | `fn :: time day of week Int Int Int` | `Int` |\n| `9` | `fn :: time utc to epoch Int Int Int Int Int Int` | `Int` |\n| `10` | `fn :: tm z era Int` | `Int` |\n| `11` | `fn :: tm mp to month Int` | `Int` |\n| `12` | `fn :: tm finish year Int Int` | `Int` |\n| `13` | `fn :: tm civil from days Int Int Int Int` | `Int Int Int Int Int Int` |\n| `14` | `fn :: time epoch to utc Int` | `Int Int Int Int Int Int` |\n| `15` | `fn :: tm pad2 Int` | `String` |\n| `16` | `fn :: tm pad4 Int` | `String` |\n| `17` | `fn :: tm fmt utc tuple (Int Int Int Int Int Int)` | `String` |\n| `18` | `fn :: time format iso8601 Int` | `String` |\n| `19` | `fn :: tm all digits String Int Int` | `Bool` |\n| `20` | `fn :: tm is sep at String Int Int Int` | `Bool` |\n| `21` | `fn :: tm parse iso8601 len String Int` | `Int` |\n| `22` | `fn :: time parse iso8601 String` | `Int` |\n| `23` | `fn :: tm join part String Int String` | `String` |\n| `24` | `fn :: time format duration ms Int` | `String` |\n| `25` | `fn :: tm pow2 loop Int Int Int` | `Int` |\n| `26` | `fn :: time backoff ms Int Int Int` | `Int` |\n\n---\n\n## 18.3. 2026-10-22T10:20:59Z\"\n        _ = assert int equals (time parse iso8601 iso) ts \"AC2: parse roundtrip\"\n        tup = time epoch to utc ts\n        _ = assert int equals (utc y tup) 2026 \"AC1: year 2026\"\n        _ = assert int equals (utc m tup) 10 \"AC1: month 10\"\n        _ = assert int equals (utc d tup) 22 \"AC1: day 22\"\n        _ = assert int equals (utc hh tup) 10 \"AC1: hour 10\"\n        _ = assert int equals (utc mm tup) 20 \"AC1: minute 20\"\n        _ = assert int equals (utc ss tup) 59 \"AC1: second 59\"\n\n        -- Negative validation on invalid calendar dates and timestamps\n        _ = assert int equals (time parse iso8601 \"2026-13-01T00:00:00Z\") (0 - 1) \"AC2: invalid month 13 -> -1\"\n        _ = assert int equals (time parse iso8601 \"2026-00-01T00:00:00Z\") (0 - 1) \"AC2: invalid month 00 -> -1\"\n        _ = assert int equals (time parse iso8601 \"2026-02-29T00:00:00Z\") (0 - 1) \"AC2: 2026-02-29 non-leap -> -1\"\n        _ = assert true ((time parse iso8601 \"2024-02-29T00:00:00Z\") > 0) \"AC2: 2024-02-29 leap year valid\"\n        _ = assert int equals (time parse iso8601 \"2026-04-31T00:00:00Z\") (0 - 1) \"AC2: April 31 -> -1\"\n        _ = assert int equals (time parse iso8601 \"2026-01-01T24:00:00Z\") (0 - 1) \"AC2: hour 24 -> -1\"\n        _ = assert int equals (time parse iso8601 \"2026-01-01T00:60:00Z\") (0 - 1) \"AC2: minute 60 -> -1\"\n        _ = assert int equals (time parse iso8601 \"2026-01-01T00:00:60Z\") (0 - 1) \"AC2: second 60 -> -1\"\n        _ = assert int equals (time parse iso8601 \"malformed\") (0 - 1) \"AC2: malformed -> -1\"\n        ()\n```\n\n### 18.3.4. Example 4: Human-Readable Duration Formatting/Parsing (`1d2h3m4s500ms`) and Capped Exponential Backoff (`time exp backoff ms`)\n\nIn this hands-on walkthrough we thoroughly exercise how `time` behaves across this functional area, covering standard inputs, parameter combinations, and corner cases:\n\n```ridgun\nfn :: test durations and backoff => ()\n      test durations and backoff =\n        _ = assert str eq (time format duration ms 0) \"0ms\" \"AC3: 0ms\"\n        _ = assert str eq (time format duration ms (0 - 50)) \"0ms\" \"AC3: negative ms -> 0ms\"\n        _ = assert str eq (time format duration ms 450) \"450ms\" \"AC3: 450ms\"\n        _ = assert str eq (time format duration ms 1000) \"1s\" \"AC3: exact 1s omits 0ms\"\n        _ = assert str eq (time format duration ms 60000) \"1m\" \"AC3: exact 1m\"\n        _ = assert str eq (time format duration ms 3600000) \"1h\" \"AC3: exact 1h\"\n        _ = assert str eq (time format duration ms 3661005) \"1h 1m 1s 5ms\" \"AC3: 1h 1m 1s 5ms\"\n\n        _ = assert int equals (time backoff ms (0 - 1) 100 5000) 100 \"AC3: negative attempt -> base_ms\"\n        _ = assert int equals (time backoff ms 0 100 5000) 100 \"AC3: attempt 0 -> 100\"\n        _ = assert int equals (time backoff ms 1 100 5000) 200 \"AC3: attempt 1 -> 200\"\n        _ = assert int equals (time backoff ms 3 100 5000) 800 \"AC3: attempt 3 -> 800\"\n        _ = assert int equals (time backoff ms 10 100 5000) 5000 \"AC3: clamped at max_ms 5000\"\n        _ = assert int equals (time backoff ms 3 0 5000) 0 \"AC3: base_ms <= 0 -> 0\"\n        _ = assert int equals (time backoff ms 3 500 200) 500 \"AC3: max_ms <= base_ms -> base_ms\"\n        ()\n```\n\n### 18.3.5. Example 5: Retry Scheduler with Exponential Backoff and ISO-8601 Audit Windows (`time parse iso8601`, `time format duration`, `time exp backoff ms`)\n\nIn this hands-on walkthrough we thoroughly exercise how `time` behaves across this functional area, covering standard inputs, parameter combinations, and corner cases:\n\n```ridgun\nimport time\n\nfn :: planificar ventana reintentos (iso_inicio : String) => (String, String, Int, Int)\n      planificar ventana reintentos iso_inicio =\n        epoch_0  = time parse iso8601 iso_inicio\n        espera_1 = time exp backoff ms 0 200 10000\n        espera_4 = time exp backoff ms 4 200 10000\n        dur_txt  = time format duration (espera_1 + espera_4)\n        iso_fin  = time format iso8601 (epoch_0 + 3600)\n        (iso_fin, dur_txt, espera_1, espera_4)\n```\n\n---\n\n## 18.4. Under the Hood: How `time` Is Built in Pure Ridgun (`src/main/ridgun/lib/time.rg`)\n\nOne of the biggest advantages of `ridgun-libs` is that **there are no C black boxes**. You can open `src/main/ridgun/lib/time.rg` and read exactly how every operation is implemented using pattern matching, `|` / `|.` guard trees, and tail-call optimization (TCO). Let's inspect the key building blocks of its real implementation:\n\n### 18.4.1. Internal Implementation: Leap Year, Month Length & Day of Week\n\nObserve how `time.rg` declares explicit `fn :: ... => ...` contracts first and resolves each case through aligned equations and tail calls:\n\n```ridgun\nfn :: time is leap year Int  => Bool\n      time is leap year year =\n        | (year % 400) == 0 = True\n        | (year % 100) == 0 = False\n        | (year % 4) == 0   = True\n        | otherwise         = False\n\nfn :: tm feb days Int  => Int\n      tm feb days year =\n        | time is leap year year = 29\n        | otherwise              = 28\n\nfn :: time days in month Int Int => Int\n      time days in month year month =\n        | month < 1 or month > 12                                      = 0\n        | month == 2                                                   = tm feb days year\n        | month == 4 or month == 6 or month == 9 or month == 11        = 30\n        | otherwise                                                    = 31\n```\n\n### 18.4.2. Internal Implementation: Howard Hinnant Civil Calendar <-> Epoch Days\n\nObserve how `time.rg` declares explicit `fn :: ... => ...` contracts first and resolves each case through aligned equations and tail calls:\n\n```ridgun\nfn :: tm adj year Int Int => Int\n      tm adj year y   m   =\n        | m <= 2    = y - 1\n        | otherwise = y\n\nfn :: tm calc era Int   => Int\n      tm calc era y_adj =\n        | y_adj >= 0 = y_adj / 400\n        | otherwise  = (y_adj - 399) / 400\n\nfn :: tm calc mp Int => Int\n      tm calc mp m   =\n        | m > 2     = m - 3\n        | otherwise = m + 9\n\nfn :: tm days from civil Int Int Int => Int\n      tm days from civil y m d =\n        y_adj = tm adj year y m\n        era   = tm calc era y_adj\n        yoe   = y_adj - (era * 400)\n        mp    = tm calc mp m\n        doy   = (((153 * mp) + 2) / 5) + d - 1\n        doe   = (yoe * 365) + (yoe / 4) - (yoe / 100) + doy\n        (era * 146097) + doe - 719468\n\nfn :: time day of week Int Int Int => Int\n      time day of week year month day =\n        z = tm days from civil year month day\n        (((z + 4) % 7) + 7) % 7\n\nfn :: time utc to epoch Int Int Int Int Int Int => Int\n      time utc to epoch y m d hh mm ss =\n        | m < 1 or m > 12                          = 0 - 1\n        | d < 1 or d > (time days in month y m)    = 0 - 1\n        | hh < 0 or hh > 23                        = 0 - 1\n        | mm < 0 or mm > 59                        = 0 - 1\n        | ss < 0 or ss > 59                        = 0 - 1\n        | otherwise =\n          days = tm days from civil y m d\n          (days * 86400) + (hh * 3600) + (mm * 60) + ss\n\nfn :: tm z era Int     => Int\n      tm z era z_shift =\n        | z_shift >= 0 = z_shift / 146097\n        | otherwise    = (z_shift - 146096) / 146097\n\nfn :: tm mp to month Int => Int\n      tm mp to month mp  =\n        | mp < 10   = mp + 3\n        | otherwise = mp - 9\n```\n\n### 18.4.3. Internal Implementation: ISO-8601 / RFC 3339 Formatting & Parsing\n\nObserve how `time.rg` declares explicit `fn :: ... => ...` contracts first and resolves each case through aligned equations and tail calls:\n\n```ridgun\nfn :: tm pad2 Int => String\n      tm pad2 n   =\n        | n < 10    = \"0\" + (int to str n)\n        | otherwise = int to str n\n\nfn :: tm pad4 Int => String\n      tm pad4 n   =\n        | n < 10   = \"000\" + (int to str n)\n        | n < 100  = \"00\" + (int to str n)\n        | n < 1000 = \"0\" + (int to str n)\n        | otherwise = int to str n\n\nfn :: tm fmt utc tuple (Int Int Int Int Int Int) => String\n      tm fmt utc tuple (y, m, d, hh, mm, ss)     =\n        (tm pad4 y) + \"-\" + (tm pad2 m) + \"-\" + (tm pad2 d) + \"T\" + (tm pad2 hh) + \":\" + (tm pad2 mm) + \":\" + (tm pad2 ss) + \"Z\"\n\nfn :: time format iso8601 Int       => String\n      time format iso8601 epoch_sec =  tm fmt utc tuple (time epoch to utc epoch_sec)\n\nfn :: tm all digits String Int Int => Bool\n      tm all digits s idx end_idx =\n        | idx >= end_idx                                       = True\n        | (str char at s idx) < 48 or (str char at s idx) > 57 = False\n        | otherwise                                            = tm all digits s (idx + 1) end_idx\n\nfn :: tm is sep at String Int Int Int => Bool\n      tm is sep at s idx c1 c2 =\n        ch = str char at s idx\n        (ch == c1) or (ch == c2)\n```\n\n### 18.4.4. Internal Implementation: Human-Readable Duration & Exponential Backoff\n\nObserve how `time.rg` declares explicit `fn :: ... => ...` contracts first and resolves each case through aligned equations and tail calls:\n\n```ridgun\nfn :: tm join part String Int String => String\n      tm join part acc val unit =\n        | val <= 0           = acc\n        | (str len acc) == 0 = (int to str val) + unit\n        | otherwise          = acc + \" \" + (int to str val) + unit\n\nfn :: time format duration ms Int => String\n      time format duration ms ms  =\n        | ms <= 0 = \"0ms\"\n        | otherwise =\n          h  = ms / 3600000\n          m  = (ms % 3600000) / 60000\n          s  = (ms % 60000) / 1000\n          rm = ms % 1000\n          p1 = tm join part \"\" h \"h\"\n          p2 = tm join part p1 m \"m\"\n          p3 = tm join part p2 s \"s\"\n          tm join part p3 rm \"ms\"\n\nfn :: tm pow2 loop Int Int Int => Int\n      tm pow2 loop rem cur max_ms =\n        | rem <= 0 or cur >= max_ms =\n          | cur > max_ms = max_ms\n          | otherwise    = cur |.\n        | otherwise = tm pow2 loop (rem - 1) (cur * 2) max_ms\n\nfn :: time backoff ms Int Int Int => Int\n      time backoff ms attempt base_ms max_ms =\n        | base_ms <= 0              = 0\n        | max_ms <= base_ms         = base_ms\n        | attempt <= 0              = base_ms\n        | otherwise                 = tm pow2 loop attempt base_ms max_ms\n```\n\n---\n\n## 18.5. Algorithmic Guarantees, `RMM1`/`RMM2` Memory, and the 5 Golden Rules of `time`\n\n- **Algorithmic & Safety Guarantee**: $O(1)$ calendar conversion with zero year-by-year loops and strict per-month day validation (rejecting Feb 29 on non-leap years).\n- **$O(1)$ Stack Complexity via TCO**: Every recursive loop in `src/main/ridgun/lib/time.rg` is in tail position, so `ridgunc` compiles them into native jumps with zero stack growth.\n- **Deterministic Memory (`RMM1`/`RMM2`)**: Zero garbage-collector pauses; all intermediate allocations are reclaimed deterministically on scope exit.\n- **Continuous Verification**: Statically checked via `stable check` and tested by the automated suite in `src/test/ridgun/test time.rg`.\n\n### The 5 Golden Rules When Programming with `time`\n\n1. **Declare `import time` at the top of your file** and take advantage of space/underscore equivalence so your code reads like natural prose.\n2. **Align your data pipelines with `=`**: chain `time` calls into named intermediate steps and let `stable format` align the columns.\n3. **Leverage `time`'s total functions inside `|` guards**: inspect return values or unwrap ADTs via pattern matching rather than assuming external input is valid.\n4. **Avoid cloning large structures when only reading**: remember that under `RMM2`, function input arguments incur zero reference-count overhead (`+0` / `-0`).\n5. **Pair `time` with `test` (`import test`)**: always lock in your domain invariants with `assert equals` or `assert true`.\n\n---\n\nIn **Chapter 19**, we will explore the **`uuid`** lib in full depth (The `uuid` Lib — RFC 9562 `UUIDv4`/`UUIDv7`, Checksums (`CRC-32`), and PRNG).\n"}, {"num": "18", "slug": "19-lib-uuid-identifiers-checksums-and-prng", "file": "19-lib-uuid-identifiers-checksums-and-prng.md", "title": "Chapter 19: The `uuid` Lib — RFC 9562 `UUIDv4`/`UUIDv7`, Checksums (`CRC-32`), and PRNG", "short_title": "The `uuid` Lib — RFC 9562 `UUIDv4`/`UUIDv7`, Checksums (`CRC-32`), and PRNG", "markdown": "# Chapter 19: The `uuid` Lib — RFC 9562 `UUIDv4`/`UUIDv7`, Checksums (`CRC-32`), and PRNG\n\n## 19.1. Why Does the `uuid` Lib Exist? (The Real-World Problem & Ridgun Philosophy)\n\n> *\"Why do I need a `uuid` lib written from scratch in pure Ridgun instead of just linking a legacy C library?\"*\n\nThat is the first question every systems engineer asks when meeting `uuid`. And the answer captures the entire philosophy of `ridgun-libs`:\n\nLet's be honest: using random `UUIDv4` values as database primary keys fragments B-Tree indexes, while rolling ad-hoc IDs without kernel CSPRNG entropy leads to collisions.\n\nThe **`uuid`** lib implements **RFC 9562 `UUIDv4`** (kernel CSPRNG) and time-ordered **`UUIDv7`** (48-bit millisecond prefix for B-Tree locality), fast checksums (**FNV-1a**, **Adler-32**, **IEEE 802.3 CRC-32**), and a deterministic seeded PRNG.\n\nIn a traditional ecosystem, solving **`UUID`** tasks means pulling in external C dependencies, wrestling with raw pointers, opaque type conversions, unchecked exceptions, or unpredictable garbage-collector pauses. In Ridgun, the **`uuid`** lib (`src/main/ridgun/lib/uuid.rg`) tackles the problem with four non-negotiable principles:\n\n1. **Total Functions & Explicit Contracts (`=>`)**: no function in `uuid` throws hidden exceptions or relies on `null`. Every edge case (empty inputs, zero divisors, truncated frames, or out-of-bounds indices) returns a documented sentinel value or an algebraic sum type (`Option`, `Result`).\n2. **Zero C Code & Zero `libc`**: every line of `uuid` compiles directly into native ELF64 machine code verified by `ridgunc`.\n3. **Tail-Call Optimization (TCO)**: all internal loops in `uuid` are tail-recursive and compile into native hardware loops using $O(1)$ stack space.\n4. **Deterministic Memory (`RMM1` / `RMM2`)**: every intermediate allocation is reclaimed at the exact instruction where its last read finishes, with no Garbage Collector.\n\n---\n\n## 19.2. Native Artifacts, Imports, and Complete `uuid` API Catalog\n\nIn `ridgun-libs`, **`uuid`** is a first-class **lib** located at `src/main/ridgun/lib/uuid.rg` (domain `UUID`). When built with `stable bundle`, it compiles into three native artifacts:\n\n| Artifact | Path | Purpose |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Ridgun Bundle** | `target/bundles/uuid.rglib` | Portable distribution and direct `import uuid` resolution |\n| **Compiled Archive** | `target/uuid.rgc` | Type-checked intermediate artifact verified by `ridgunc` |\n| **ELF64 Shared Object** | `target/libuuid.so` | Native dynamic shared library with zero C or `libc` dependencies |\n\n> To use this lib in any `.rg` file, simply add `import uuid` at the top and call its functions using either multi-word spaces (`my fn x`) or underscores (`my_fn x`).\n\n### 19.2.1. Summary of `uuid` Functional Families\n\n| Signature / Function / Type | Purpose & Usage |\n|---|---|\n| `uuid v4 / uuid v7 / uuid v7 at` | Generate RFC 9562 v4 (random) and v7 (time-ordered) canonical 36-char UUIDs |\n| `uuid is valid / uuid version` | Validate 8-4-4-4-12 layout and RFC 9562 variant bits (`8, 9, a, b`) |\n| `fnv1a 32 / adler32 / crc32 / crc32 hex` | Fast 32-bit non-cryptographic hashes and checksums |\n| `prng next / prng range` | Pure functional Linear Congruential PRNG for reproducible tests and simulations |\n\n### 19.2.2. Exhaustive Catalog of Types and Signatures in `src/main/ridgun/lib/uuid.rg`\n\nBelow is the complete inventory of algebraic data types (`type`), mixfix operators (`op ::`), and function signatures (`fn ::`) defined in `src/main/ridgun/lib/uuid.rg` (`0` types, `0` operators, and `33` functions):\n\n| # | Function / Signature (`fn ::`) | Return Type |\n|---|---|---|\n| `1` | `fn :: uu hex lut Int` | `String` |\n| `2` | `fn :: uu u32 Int` | `Int` |\n| `3` | `fn :: uu bxor loop Int Int Int Int` | `Int` |\n| `4` | `fn :: uu bxor32 Int Int` | `Int` |\n| `5` | `fn :: uu fnv loop String Int Int Int` | `Int` |\n| `6` | `fn :: fnv1a 32 String` | `Int` |\n| `7` | `fn :: uu adler loop String Int Int Int Int` | `Int` |\n| `8` | `fn :: adler32 String` | `Int` |\n| `9` | `fn :: uu crc bit loop Int Int` | `Int` |\n| `10` | `fn :: uu crc byte loop String Int Int Int` | `Int` |\n| `11` | `fn :: crc32 String` | `Int` |\n| `12` | `fn :: uu abs Int` | `Int` |\n| `13` | `fn :: prng next Int` | `Int` |\n| `14` | `fn :: uu prng range step Int Int Int` | `Int Int` |\n| `15` | `fn :: prng range Int Int Int` | `Int Int` |\n| `16` | `fn :: uu alloc page Int` | `Int` |\n| `17` | `fn :: uu free page Int Int Int` | `Int` |\n| `18` | `fn :: uu alloc mfd` | `Int` |\n| `19` | `fn :: uu peek Int Int Int` | `String` |\n| `20` | `fn :: uu decode u32 le String Int` | `Int` |\n| `21` | `fn :: uu decode u64 le String Int` | `Int` |\n| `22` | `fn :: uu wall time ms` | `Int` |\n| `23` | `fn :: uu bytes to hex loop String Int Int` | `String` |\n| `24` | `fn :: uu random hex16` | `String` |\n| `25` | `fn :: uu hex val Int` | `Int` |\n| `26` | `fn :: uu variant char String` | `String` |\n| `27` | `fn :: uuid v4` | `String` |\n| `28` | `fn :: uu hex48 loop Int Int` | `String` |\n| `29` | `fn :: uuid v7 at Int` | `String` |\n| `30` | `fn :: uuid v7` | `String` |\n| `31` | `fn :: uu is hex range String Int Int` | `Bool` |\n| `32` | `fn :: uuid is valid String` | `Bool` |\n| `33` | `fn :: uuid version String` | `Int` |\n\n---\n\n## 19.3. \"not-a-uuid\") (0 - 1) \"AC1: invalid uuid version -> -1\"\n        ()\n```\n\n### 19.3.4. Example 4: FNV-1a, DJB2, Adler-32, IEEE 802.3 CRC32 (`0xCBF43926`) Checksums, and Deterministic SplitMix64 PRNG\n\nIn this hands-on walkthrough we thoroughly exercise how `uuid` behaves across this functional area, covering standard inputs, parameter combinations, and corner cases:\n\n```ridgun\nfn :: test checksums and prng => ()\n      test checksums and prng =\n        -- Official FNV-1a 32-bit vectors (isthe.com/chongo/tech/comp/fnv/)\n        _ = assert int equals (fnv1a 32 \"\") 2166136261 \"AC2: FNV-1a 32 '' == 0x811c9dc5 (offset basis)\"\n        _ = assert int equals (fnv1a 32 \"a\") 3826002220 \"AC2: FNV-1a 32 'a' == 0xe40c292c\"\n        _ = assert int equals (fnv1a 32 \"foobar\") 3214735720 \"AC2: FNV-1a 32 'foobar' == 0xbf9cf968\"\n\n        -- Official RFC 1950 Adler-32 vectors\n        _ = assert int equals (adler32 \"\") 1 \"AC2: Adler-32 '' == 1\"\n        _ = assert int equals (adler32 \"a\") 6422626 \"AC2: Adler-32 'a' == 0x00620062\"\n        _ = assert int equals (adler32 \"Wikipedia\") 300286872 \"AC2: Adler-32 'Wikipedia' == 0x11e60398\"\n\n        -- Official IEEE 802.3 / ISO-HDLC CRC-32 vectors\n        _ = assert int equals (crc32 \"\") 0 \"AC2: CRC-32 '' == 0\"\n        _ = assert int equals (crc32 \"a\") 3904355907 \"AC2: CRC-32 'a' == 0xe8b7be43\"\n        _ = assert int equals (crc32 \"123456789\") 3421780262 \"AC2: CRC-32 '123456789' == 0xcbf43926\"\n\n        -- Deterministic 31-bit LCG PRNG\n        s1 = prng next 42\n        s2 = prng next 42\n        s3 = prng next s1\n        _ = assert int equals s1 s2 \"AC3: prng_next deterministic for same seed\"\n        _ = assert false (s1 == s3) \"AC3: prng_next advances state\"\n        r1 = prng range 42 10 20\n        v1 = prng val r1\n        _ = assert true (v1 >= 10 and v1 <= 20) \"AC3: prng_range within [10, 20]\"\n        r_deg = prng range 42 50 10\n        _ = assert int equals (prng val r_deg) 50 \"AC3: prng_range with hi <= lo clamps to lo\"\n        ()\n```\n\n### 19.3.5. Under the Hood: How `uuid` Is Built in Pure Ridgun (`src/main/ridgun/lib/uuid.rg`)\n\nOne of the biggest advantages of `ridgun-libs` is that **there are no C black boxes**. You can open `src/main/ridgun/lib/uuid.rg` and read exactly how every operation is implemented using pattern matching, `|` / `|.` guard trees, and tail-call optimization (TCO). Let's inspect the key building blocks of its real implementation:\n\n### 19.4.1. Internal Implementation: RFC 9562 UUIDv4 & UUIDv7 Generation & Validation (CSPRNG + clock_gettime)\n\nObserve how `uuid.rg` declares explicit `fn :: ... => ...` contracts first and resolves each case through aligned equations and tail calls:\n\n```ridgun\nfn :: uu alloc page Int => Int\n      uu alloc page sz  =\n        neg1 = 0 - 1\n        sys call 9 0 sz 3 34 neg1 0\n\nfn :: uu free page Int Int Int => Int\n      uu free page mfd ptr sz =\n        _ = sys close mfd\n        sys call 11 ptr sz 0 0 0 0\n\nfn :: uu alloc mfd => Int\n      uu alloc mfd =  sys call 319 4194311 1 0 0 0 0\n\nfn :: uu peek Int Int Int => String\n      uu peek mfd ptr len =\n        _ = sys call 18 mfd ptr len 0 0 0\n        _ = sys call 8 mfd 0 0 0 0 0\n        sys read fd mfd len\n\nfn :: uu decode u32 le String Int => Int\n      uu decode u32 le s off =\n        b0 = str char at s off\n        b1 = str char at s (off + 1)\n        b2 = str char at s (off + 2)\n        b3 = str char at s (off + 3)\n        b0 + (b1 * 256) + (b2 * 65536) + (b3 * 16777216)\n\nfn :: uu decode u64 le String Int => Int\n      uu decode u64 le s off =\n        lo = uu decode u32 le s off\n        hi = uu decode u32 le s (off + 4)\n        lo + (hi * 4294967296)\n\nfn :: uu wall time ms => Int\n      uu wall time ms =\n        ptr  = uu alloc page 4096\n        mfd  = uu alloc mfd\n        _    = sys call 228 0 ptr 0 0 0 0\n        raw  = uu peek mfd ptr 16\n        _    = uu free page mfd ptr 4096\n        sec  = uu decode u64 le raw 0\n        nsec = uu decode u64 le raw 8\n        (sec * 1000) + (nsec / 1000000)\n```\n\n---\n\n## 19.5. Algorithmic Guarantees, `RMM1`/`RMM2` Memory, and the 5 Golden Rules of `uuid`\n\n- **Algorithmic & Safety Guarantee**: `uuid v7 at t1 < uuid v7 at t2` guarantees monotonically increasing lexicographical order over time, ideal for `btree` indexes and databases.\n- **$O(1)$ Stack Complexity via TCO**: Every recursive loop in `src/main/ridgun/lib/uuid.rg` is in tail position, so `ridgunc` compiles them into native jumps with zero stack growth.\n- **Deterministic Memory (`RMM1`/`RMM2`)**: Zero garbage-collector pauses; all intermediate allocations are reclaimed deterministically on scope exit.\n- **Continuous Verification**: Statically checked via `stable check` and tested by the automated suite in `src/test/ridgun/test uuid.rg`.\n\n### The 5 Golden Rules When Programming with `uuid`\n\n1. **Declare `import uuid` at the top of your file** and take advantage of space/underscore equivalence so your code reads like natural prose.\n2. **Align your data pipelines with `=`**: chain `uuid` calls into named intermediate steps and let `stable format` align the columns.\n3. **Leverage `uuid`'s total functions inside `|` guards**: inspect return values or unwrap ADTs via pattern matching rather than assuming external input is valid.\n4. **Avoid cloning large structures when only reading**: remember that under `RMM2`, function input arguments incur zero reference-count overhead (`+0` / `-0`).\n5. **Pair `uuid` with `test` (`import test`)**: always lock in your domain invariants with `assert equals` or `assert true`.\n\n---\n\nIn **Chapter 20**, we will explore the **`log`** lib in full depth (The `log` Lib — Structured NDJSON Logging and Automatic Secret Redaction).\n"}, {"num": "19", "slug": "20-lib-log-structured-ndjson-and-redaction", "file": "20-lib-log-structured-ndjson-and-redaction.md", "title": "Chapter 20: The `log` Lib — Structured NDJSON Logging and Automatic Secret Redaction", "short_title": "The `log` Lib — Structured NDJSON Logging and Automatic Secret Redaction", "markdown": "# Chapter 20: The `log` Lib — Structured NDJSON Logging and Automatic Secret Redaction\n\n## 20.1. Why Does the `log` Lib Exist? (The Real-World Problem & Ridgun Philosophy)\n\n> *\"Why do I need a `log` lib written from scratch in pure Ridgun instead of just linking a legacy C library?\"*\n\nThat is the first question every systems engineer asks when meeting `log`. And the answer captures the entire philosophy of `ridgun-libs`:\n\nLet's be honest: dumping request fields into a log line and realizing weeks later that `password`, `api_key`, or `authorization` headers were stored in cleartext in your log aggregator is a classic security incident.\n\nThe **`log`** lib provides severity filtering (`DEBUG`, `INFO`, `WARN`, `ERROR`), human-readable text and **structured NDJSON** formatters, and **automatic secret redaction** (`password`, `secret`, `token`, `api_key`, `authorization`, `cookie`, `private_key` $\\rightarrow$ `\"[REDACTED]\"`, mitigating `VUL-LOG-001`).\n\nIn a traditional ecosystem, solving **`LOG`** tasks means pulling in external C dependencies, wrestling with raw pointers, opaque type conversions, unchecked exceptions, or unpredictable garbage-collector pauses. In Ridgun, the **`log`** lib (`src/main/ridgun/lib/log.rg`) tackles the problem with four non-negotiable principles:\n\n1. **Total Functions & Explicit Contracts (`=>`)**: no function in `log` throws hidden exceptions or relies on `null`. Every edge case (empty inputs, zero divisors, truncated frames, or out-of-bounds indices) returns a documented sentinel value or an algebraic sum type (`Option`, `Result`).\n2. **Zero C Code & Zero `libc`**: every line of `log` compiles directly into native ELF64 machine code verified by `ridgunc`.\n3. **Tail-Call Optimization (TCO)**: all internal loops in `log` are tail-recursive and compile into native hardware loops using $O(1)$ stack space.\n4. **Deterministic Memory (`RMM1` / `RMM2`)**: every intermediate allocation is reclaimed at the exact instruction where its last read finishes, with no Garbage Collector.\n\n---\n\n## 20.2. Native Artifacts, Imports, and Complete `log` API Catalog\n\nIn `ridgun-libs`, **`log`** is a first-class **lib** located at `src/main/ridgun/lib/log.rg` (domain `LOG`). When built with `stable bundle`, it compiles into three native artifacts:\n\n| Artifact | Path | Purpose |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Ridgun Bundle** | `target/bundles/log.rglib` | Portable distribution and direct `import log` resolution |\n| **Compiled Archive** | `target/log.rgc` | Type-checked intermediate artifact verified by `ridgunc` |\n| **ELF64 Shared Object** | `target/liblog.so` | Native dynamic shared library with zero C or `libc` dependencies |\n\n> To use this lib in any `.rg` file, simply add `import log` at the top and call its functions using either multi-word spaces (`my fn x`) or underscores (`my_fn x`).\n\n### 20.2.1. Summary of `log` Functional Families\n\n| Signature / Function / Type | Purpose & Usage |\n|---|---|\n| `log level rank / log should emit` | Compare log levels (`DEBUG=10`, `INFO=20`, `WARN=30`, `ERROR=40`) against configured threshold |\n| `log is sensitive key / log redact fields` | Case-insensitively replace secret values with `\"[REDACTED]\"` |\n| `log format text / log format json` | Format single-line key=value text or RFC 8259 NDJSON with automatic secret redaction |\n\n### 20.2.2. Exhaustive Catalog of Types and Signatures in `src/main/ridgun/lib/log.rg`\n\nBelow is the complete inventory of algebraic data types (`type`), mixfix operators (`op ::`), and function signatures (`fn ::`) defined in `src/main/ridgun/lib/log.rg` (`0` types, `0` operators, and `21` functions):\n\n| # | Function / Signature (`fn ::`) | Return Type |\n|---|---|---|\n| `1` | `fn :: lg lower char String Int Int` | `String` |\n| `2` | `fn :: lg to lower loop String Int Int` | `String` |\n| `3` | `fn :: lg to lower String` | `String` |\n| `4` | `fn :: lg rank from lower String` | `Int` |\n| `5` | `fn :: log level rank String` | `Int` |\n| `6` | `fn :: log level name Int` | `String` |\n| `7` | `fn :: log should emit String String` | `Bool` |\n| `8` | `fn :: lg check sens lower String` | `Bool` |\n| `9` | `fn :: log is sensitive key String` | `Bool` |\n| `10` | `fn :: lg pair key (String String)` | `String` |\n| `11` | `fn :: lg pair val (String String)` | `String` |\n| `12` | `fn :: lg redact pair (String String)` | `String String` |\n| `13` | `fn :: log redact fields [String String]` | `[String String]` |\n| `14` | `fn :: lg esc char String Int Int` | `String` |\n| `15` | `fn :: lg esc loop String Int Int` | `String` |\n| `16` | `fn :: lg esc str String` | `String` |\n| `17` | `fn :: lg quote String` | `String` |\n| `18` | `fn :: lg fmt text fields [String String]` | `String` |\n| `19` | `fn :: log format text String String [String String]` | `String` |\n| `20` | `fn :: lg fmt json fields [String String]` | `String` |\n| `21` | `fn :: log format json String String [String String]` | `String` |\n\n---\n\n## 20.3. Under the Hood: How `log` Is Built in Pure Ridgun (`src/main/ridgun/lib/log.rg`)\n\nOne of the biggest advantages of `ridgun-libs` is that **there are no C black boxes**. Let's inspect the key building blocks of its real implementation:\n\n### 20.4.1. Internal Implementation: Severity Levels (`DEBUG`, `INFO`, `WARN`, `ERROR`)\n\nObserve how `log.rg` declares explicit `fn :: ... => ...` contracts first and resolves each case through aligned equations and tail calls:\n\n```ridgun\nfn :: lg rank from lower String => Int\n      lg rank from lower lv     =\n        | lv == \"debug\"                       = 10\n        | lv == \"info\"                        = 20\n        | lv == \"warn\" or lv == \"warning\"     = 30\n        | lv == \"error\" or lv == \"err\"        = 40\n        | otherwise                           = 20\n\nfn :: log level rank String => Int\n      log level rank level  =  lg rank from lower (lg to lower level)\n\nfn :: log level name Int  => String\n      log level name rank =\n        | rank <= 10 = \"DEBUG\"\n        | rank <= 20 = \"INFO\"\n        | rank <= 30 = \"WARN\"\n        | otherwise  = \"ERROR\"\n\nfn :: log should emit String    String    => Bool\n      log should emit min_level msg_level =  (log level rank msg_level) >= (log level rank min_level)\n```\n\n### 20.4.2. Internal Implementation: Sensitive Key Detection & Redaction\n\nObserve how `log.rg` declares explicit `fn :: ... => ...` contracts first and resolves each case through aligned equations and tail calls:\n\n```ridgun\nfn :: lg check sens lower String => Bool\n      lg check sens lower lk     =\n        | str contains lk \"password\" or str contains lk \"passwd\"       = True\n        | str contains lk \"secret\" or str contains lk \"token\"          = True\n        | str contains lk \"api_key\" or str contains lk \"api-key\"       = True\n        | str contains lk \"apikey\"                                     = True\n        | str contains lk \"authorization\" or str contains lk \"cookie\"  = True\n        | str contains lk \"private_key\" or str contains lk \"private-key\" = True\n        | otherwise                                                    = False\n\nfn :: log is sensitive key String => Bool\n      log is sensitive key key    =  lg check sens lower (lg to lower key)\n\nfn :: lg pair key (String String) => String\n      lg pair key (k, _)          =  k\n\nfn :: lg pair val (String String) => String\n      lg pair val (_, v)          =  v\n\nfn :: lg redact pair (String String) => String String\n      lg redact pair (k, v)          =\n        | log is sensitive key k = (k, \"[REDACTED]\")\n        | otherwise              = (k, v)\n\nfn :: log redact fields [String String] => [String String]\n      log redact fields []              =  []\n      log redact fields (pair:rest)     =  [(lg redact pair pair)] :: (log redact fields rest)\n```\n\n### 20.4.3. Internal Implementation: Escaping & Text / NDJSON Formatting\n\nObserve how `log.rg` declares explicit `fn :: ... => ...` contracts first and resolves each case through aligned equations and tail calls:\n\n```ridgun\nfn :: lg esc char String Int Int => String\n      lg esc char s idx c =\n        | c == 34   = \"\\\\\\\"\"\n        | c == 92   = \"\\\\\\\\\"\n        | c == 10   = \"\\\\n\"\n        | c == 13   = \"\\\\r\"\n        | c == 9    = \"\\\\t\"\n        | otherwise = str slice s idx (idx + 1)\n\nfn :: lg esc loop String Int Int => String\n      lg esc loop s idx len =\n        | len s)\n\nfn :: lg quote String => String\n      lg quote s      =  \"\\\"\" + (lg esc str s) + \"\\\"\"\n\nfn :: lg fmt text fields [String String] => String\n      lg fmt text fields []              =  \"\"\n      lg fmt text fields (pair:rest)     =\n        \" \" + (lg esc str (lg pair key pair)) + \"=\" + (lg esc str (lg pair val pair)) + (lg fmt text fields rest)\n\nfn :: log format text String String [String String] => String\n      log format text level msg fields =\n        norm_lv = log level name (log level rank level)\n        safe_f  = log redact fields fields\n        \"[\" + norm_lv + \"] \" + (lg esc str msg) + (lg fmt text fields safe_f)\n\nfn :: lg fmt json fields [String String] => String\n      lg fmt json fields []              =  \"\"\n      lg fmt json fields (pair:rest)     =\n        \",\" + (lg quote (lg pair key pair)) + \":\" + (lg quote (lg pair val pair)) + (lg fmt json fields rest)\n\nfn :: log format json String String [String String] => String\n      log format json level msg fields =\n        norm_lv = log level name (log level rank level)\n        safe_f  = log redact fields fields\n        \"{\\\"level\\\":\" + (lg quote norm_lv) + \",\\\"msg\\\":\" + (lg quote msg) + (lg fmt json fields safe_f) + \"}\"\n```\n\n---\n\n## 20.5. Algorithmic Guarantees, `RMM1`/`RMM2` Memory, and the 5 Golden Rules of `log`\n\n- **Algorithmic & Safety Guarantee**: Both `log format text` and `log format json` automatically pass fields through `log redact fields` prior to serialization, ensuring secrets never leak by accident.\n- **$O(1)$ Stack Complexity via TCO**: Every recursive loop in `src/main/ridgun/lib/log.rg` is in tail position, so `ridgunc` compiles them into native jumps with zero stack growth.\n- **Deterministic Memory (`RMM1`/`RMM2`)**: Zero garbage-collector pauses;
